A Geography of Human Rights Abuses

AbSTRAcT
page=2 block=437257230 (x=139 y=173) width=48.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=1 wordint.max=21
What can geography as a discipline contribute towards a better understand-
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=192) width=288.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing of human rights issues? Based on an analysis of three maps, selected from 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=203) width=291.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a larger set of thirty-two generated from data abstracted from 720 Amnesty 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=214) width=291.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
International Freedom Writers Letters, this study finds that the number and 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=225) width=291.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
frequency of human rights abuses over the past twenty years has remained 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=236) width=290.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stable at best. A set of ten confounding factors are indentified to explain 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=247) width=291.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
this lack of progress towards improving worldwide human rights. This article 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=258) width=290.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
concludes that geographers and human rights scholars could profit in their 
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=269) width=290.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
work by collaborating and consulting with one another’s research.
page=2 block=362366400 (x=19 y=280) width=259.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
I.  INTRodUcTIoN
page=2 block=6293346 (x=1 y=315) width=96.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Although geography as a discipline is not commonly associated with de-
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=339) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
scribing and explaining human rights abuses or providing policy remedies 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=351) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for such abuses, it would seem that many feel it definitely should be. Rex 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=363) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Honey, for example, has issued an explicit call for political geographers to 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=375) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lend their expertise to the study of human rights.1 He stresses three points in 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=386) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
his plea for geographers to engage in such research. First, geography explores 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=399) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the context in which human rights struggles occur and brings to the study of 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=411) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights two kinds of knowledge: knowledge of cultures and knowledge 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=423) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of the nature-society nexus. As such, omitting the geographical background 
page=2 block=21464850 (x=1 y=435) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
* Roger Mark Selya is Professor Emeritus of Geography and sometime Director of the Middle 
page=2 block=21380096 (x=1 y=480) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Eastern Studies Program at the University of Cincinnati. His research focus is on the ecologi-
page=2 block=21380096 (x=10 y=489) width=315.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cal, demographic, political, and spatial consequences of the modernization of East Asia and 
page=2 block=21380096 (x=10 y=498) width=317.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Middle East. 
page=2 block=21380096 (x=10 y=507) width=58.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
  1.  Rex Honey, Human Rights, in GeoGraphy in america at the Dawn of the 21st century 730

(Gary L. Gaile & Cort J. Willmott eds., 2003).

HUMAN RIGHTS QUARTERLY
page=3 block=820772870 (x=101 y=1) width=124.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Vol. 34
page=3 block=562561030 (x=296 y=1) width=29.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and consequences of human rights issues is likely to be incomplete because 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=25) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
it fails to capture both the cultural struggles over what a just society is and 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the milieu of interrelated sites of injustice. Conversely, according to Honey, 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in order for geography as a discipline to fulfill its potential as a scholarly 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
endeavor examining the human condition, human rights must be part of its 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
research focus. 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=85) width=68.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
At present, Honey notes that there are two traditions in the geographic 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=19 y=97) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
approach to human rights. An older, implicit approach has focused on hu-
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=109) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
man rights problems such as racism, slavery, social justice, poverty, and 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
electoral violations without specific reference to any extant human rights 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
doctrines or agreements. In their presidential addresses, for example, many 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
past presidents of the Association of American Geographers, such as Richard 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Hartshorne, Richard Morrill, Harold Rose, Gilbert White, and Julian Wolpert, 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
have called for the eradication of these wrongs. In contrast, the second, more 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
contemporary tradition has actively and formally advocated for democracy 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and pushed for civil and political liberties based on both the French and 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
United States Revolutions. In some cases, scholars have lobbied or advocated 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for a communal rights agenda emphasizing social and economic benefits: 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=229) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
wages, jobs, education, and health care. 
page=3 block=21435822 (x=1 y=241) width=179.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
II.  AN ANoMALY
page=3 block=5572222 (x=1 y=277) width=85.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The proliferation of reports, country studies, and documentaries issued dur-
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=301) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing the past two decades by a growing variety of national and international 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=313) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
governments and human rights organizations would suggest that there should 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=325) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be sufficient data to prepare a variety of geographic analyses of the global 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=337) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights situation. Oddly enough, only one article by a geographer 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=349) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
has ever appeared in the Human Rights Quarterly.2 This anomaly pervades 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=360) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the main professional organization of geographers, the American Associa-
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=373) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion of Geographers (AAG), which sponsors a cooperative program with 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=385) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the American Association for the Advancement of Science (AAAS). Its goal 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=397) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
is to expand an array of projects and programs to engage geographers as 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=409) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
scientists in human rights issues. As of January 2009 the two organizations 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=421) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
anticipated creating a new Science and Human Rights Coalition, establish-
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=433) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing an AAG clearinghouse on geographic research and human rights in 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=445) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cooperation with an AAAS project on geospatial technologies and human 
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=457) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rights.3 Annual meetings of the AAG now feature special sessions on hu-
page=3 block=21482646 (x=1 y=468) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

The above discussion notwithstanding, geographers have in fact pub-
page=4 block=21437022 (x=19 y=73) width=306.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lished a considerable body of literature on the subject of human rights, but 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the vast amount of it has appeared in geographic journals. For example, in 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
a focus were published between 1982 and 1991; in Political Geography, 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
between January 1992 and June 2011, some 712 articles with human rights 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as a focus were published; geographers also published some 833 human 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rights related articles in Antipod: A Radical Journal of Geography, over the 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Oblique wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
period January 1997 through June 2011. These materials demonstrate that 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
geographers have made meaningful, albeit unrecognized, contributions to 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
understanding the relationships between human rights abuses and borders, 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
development,5 environmental, ethnic, land and resource conflicts, and social 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=204) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
justice.6 Furthermore, the use of GIS and Remote Sensing has permitted the 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=216) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
documentation of where abuses have occurred even when it is difficult for 
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=229) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
observers to directly visit suspect locations.7
page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=240) width=191.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=4 block=21437022 (x=1 y=277) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
interested in human rights publish almost exclusively in geography journals,

HUMAN RIGHTS QUARTERLY
page=5 block=820772870 (x=101 y=1) width=124.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Vol. 34
page=5 block=562561030 (x=296 y=1) width=29.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
especially those that focus on political geography. Moreover, regardless of 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=25) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
where geographers publish, they rarely refer to the broader human rights 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
literature. If they do reference non-geographic materials, they are most likely 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to draw from contemporary materials in political science or international 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
relations. Finally, in many geographic papers dealing with human rights, the 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights component frequently gets lost. So for example, Nurit Kliot’s 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
article on refugees and human rights provides considerable detail regarding 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
their number and places of origin as well as their places of refuge. But the 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights aspect of the plight of the refugees is only implied.8 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=120) width=288.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The present study seeks to provide an example of a geographic analysis 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=19 y=133) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of a human rights issue using a multidisciplinary approach. By fulfilling this 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
goal it is hoped that on the one hand those in the field of human rights 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
research will consider looking to geography for insights and new paths of 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
research, and on the other hand that geographers will explicitly include the 
page=5 block=21434622 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights literature in their work.

III. AN ExAMpLE of A GEoGRApHIc ANALYSIS of A HUMAN 
page=5 block=19271706 (x=1 y=229) width=294.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
RIGHTS ISSUE
page=5 block=19271706 (x=19 y=241) width=65.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The question I wish to explore is whether or not there has been a change 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=265) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in the prevalence of the spatial patterns of human rights abuses over the 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=277) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
period 1986–2006. There are ample reasons to speculate that such changes 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=289) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
might or should have occurred. The end of the Cold War, the fall of the So-
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=301) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
viet Union, the 1997 Asian Financial Crisis, the spread of globalization and 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=313) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
economic development, and the end of the “dirty wars” in Latin America 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=325) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
have all had the potential for enhancing respect for human rights and re-
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=337) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ducing human rights abuses. So my working hypothesis will be that related 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=349) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sets of economic and political changes have in fact led to improvements in 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=361) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights, and that such improvements can be seen in an examination 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=373) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of maps showing human rights abuses over time. Possible changes in the 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=385) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
spatial distribution of human rights abuses are dealt with as follows: first, 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=397) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the data set used will be presented; then, maps of the number, percent, and 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=409) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
prevalence of human rights abuses will be described and analyzed. Finally, 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=421) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
materials that can provide an explanation for the maps and an answer to 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=433) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the question regarding possible changes in the spatial distribution of rights 
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=445) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
abuses will be presented.
page=5 block=21482742 (x=1 y=457) width=110.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
  8.  N. Kliot, The Era of Homeless Man, 72 GeoG. 109 (1987).

IV. dATA SoURcES 
page=6 block=6226070 (x=1 y=25) width=95.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Over the past twenty-five years the significant increase in the number of 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
governmental and nongovernmental organizations (NGOs) concerned with 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights situations has spawned a plethora of topical and regional 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reports devoted to the status of those rights. The same can be said of the 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
scholarly literature on human rights. However, it is widely recognized that 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
it is extremely difficult to use this massive literature to do both longitudinal 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and cross-cultural analysis due to problems regarding the accuracy, validity, 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and comparability of data, as well as the differences in scale, methodolo-
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=133) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gies, measuring systems, and data processing techniques employed in these 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reports, articles, and monographs.9 Our research uses a database with a 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=156) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
twenty year history. This database is made up of the collected model letters 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
used in the Amnesty International USA (AI) Freedom Writers initiative. Each 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
month, AI provided members of the initiative with three letters to be sent 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to heads of government or state, ministries of justice, or law enforcement 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
officials, each letter describing a specific case of human rights abuse in their 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
country. In all, there were 720 letters available for use. The letters for the 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=229) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
period September 1986 to August 2006 were accessed on 20 November 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
2006 at the Amnesty International Archive located at Columbia University.10 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=252) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Letters missing from this archive were provided by Ellen Moore, then of the AI 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=265) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Urgent Action Office, located in Nederland, Colorado. Letters for September 
page=6 block=21441738 (x=1 y=277) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

try were grouped into five periods: 1986–2006, 1986–1991, 1992–1996,

page=7 block=21439122 (x=1 y=253) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
produced. In all, thirty-two maps were generated.

Only five maps are included in this article, however. There is a strong

prevalence rate. The entire map set, as a well as a comprehensive bibliog-

V.  dEScRIpTIoN ANd ANALYSIS of THE MApS 
page=7 block=14944662 (x=1 y=409) width=228.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
At first glance the map showing the prevalence of human rights abuses for 
page=7 block=21438066 (x=1 y=433) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the entire study period (Figure 2) seems cluttered and difficult to interpret. 
page=7 block=21438066 (x=1 y=445) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
 11. 
page=7 block=1116487 (x=1 y=475) width=17.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
A location quotient (Lq) is a coefficient comparing some quality of an area with a specified 
page=7 block=494812000 (x=25 y=475) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
norm. A Lq equal to one indicates that the area in question is represented in exactly the 
page=7 block=494812000 (x=25 y=484) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
same proportion as the norm; an Lq of less than one indicates an under-representation 
page=7 block=494812000 (x=25 y=493) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
against the norm; a Lq greater than one suggest the area has more than its “fair share” 
page=7 block=494812000 (x=25 y=502) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
against the norm. See peter haGGett, locational analysis in human GeoGraphy 236 (1966); 
page=7 block=494808553 (x=25 y=511) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima-SC650 wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
r.J. Johnston, Derek GreGory & DaviD m. smith, the Dictionary of human GeoGraphy 339 
page=7 block=494808553 (x=25 y=520) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima-SC650 wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
(3d ed. 1994); DaviD m. smith, patterns in human GeoGraphy: an introDuction to numerical 
page=7 block=494808553 (x=25 y=529) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima-SC650 wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
methoDs 161–62 (1975). For Figure 1, each country’s percent of total cited abuses was 
page=7 block=494804332 (x=25 y=538) width=302.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
compared to its percent of world population.
page=7 block=494804332 (x=25 y=547) width=157.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

There appear to be few countries that were not cited at some point in time. 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=25) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In fact, a simple headcount of countries shows that seventy-two of them, 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
amounting to 37.5 percent of all the countries and territories in the world, 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
were never cited for abuses. A regional12 breakdown (Table 2) of the percent 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=60) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of countries that were accused of human rights abuses further qualifies our 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
first impression of the map. There is a wide range of what percent of each 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
region’s countries were cited. The Pacific Basin (including Australia and 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
New Zealand) and the Caribbean Basin appear relatively immune to human 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rights abuse. At the opposite end of the spectrum, the countries that make 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
up the constellation of the Former Soviet Union—Armenia, Belarus, Georgia, 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Moldova, Russia, and Ukraine—seem particularly prone to abuses. Europe 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
occupies a place slightly above the low end, while the remaining regions 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cluster towards the high end. A regional subdivision of Europe suggests that 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Eastern and Southern Europe are more prone to human rights abuses than 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Western and Northern sections. A breakdown of Asia shows that only 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
slightly more than a third of the countries in East Asia were cited, while the 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
other regions are all in the high 80 percent to 90 percent range of abuse.

Finally, by looking at the citation records for individual countries (Tables 3

and 4) it is possible to see which countries are the chronic abusers of human

rights. For each study period there appear to be on average fifteen chronic

abusers. The lists do contain repeat offenders, such as China, Myanmar, and

page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=289) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=301) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to be the most abusive region, followed by the Middle East and North Af-
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=313) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rica, Latin America, and Africa. These findings parallel those of Diamond

and Richards, both of whom argue that the geographic patterns of human 
page=11 block=21439722 (x=1 y=337) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rights performance appear to reflect major cultural or civilizational roots.13

page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=328) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Finally, the map of locational quotients of human rights prevalence (Fig-
page=12 block=21499416 (x=19 y=340) width=306.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ure 1) needs to be analyzed. In some ways this metric may be considered a 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=352) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
“fair share” approach to describing the distribution of human rights abuses, 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=364) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
since the location quotient compares a country’s share of the total number 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=376) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of abuses with its share of the total world population. We thus can get a 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=388) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sense of which countries are over or under represented in the spectrum of 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=400) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights abusers. By this standard then, thirty-two countries, including 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=412) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
China (which by the sheer number of citations can be considered a major 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=424) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rights abuser) with location quotients below 1.00 appear to have abuse rates 
page=12 block=21499416 (x=1 y=436) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

Amnesty International has identified twenty categories (Table 7). Note that

page=18 block=21482298 (x=1 y=335) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
type of abuse. Two questions can be answered from these data. First, for 
page=18 block=21482298 (x=1 y=347) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
each country what is the extent of abuse types? Unfortunately, with a few

exceptions most countries engage in multiple forms of abuse (Figure 4). The 
page=18 block=21482298 (x=1 y=371) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
most egregious examples are Turkey (seven types) and Mexico (eight types). 
page=18 block=21482298 (x=1 y=383) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Second, are there regional preferences for certain combinations of abuse

(Table 8)? For example, torture appears to be a special problem in Latin

America, where it was believed that communist subversion was being carried 
page=18 block=21482298 (x=1 y=419) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
out and had to be stopped at all costs. Although torture was known before 
page=18 block=21482298 (x=1 y=431) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the rise of authoritarian governments in the 1970s, in the1980s it was more

page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=217) width=318.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In addition to regional preferences it is important to keep in mind that 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=19 y=230) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
abuses changed over time in different areas. For example, in Africa despots 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=242) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
would use the death penalty and genocide as a means of abuse. However, 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=254) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
when civil wars broke out or during debt crises, the very existence of civil 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=266) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
strife and the breakdown of states led to refugee abuse and rape as weapons 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=278) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of war and abuse. Even the HIV/AIDS epidemic generated discrimination 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=290) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and abuse.19 In the Former Soviet Union abuse changed after 1991 from 
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=301) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
terror and torture to official regulation and discrimination.20
page=20 block=21453756 (x=1 y=313) width=259.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

A Geography of Human Rights Abuses
page=22 block=866648070 (x=87 y=1) width=152.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
1065
page=22 block=399769606 (x=305 y=1) width=20.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=1 wordint.max=18
ther the theory nor the facts supporting it are universally accepted.23 The 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=24) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
validity of the theory has been challenged for several reasons. First, the 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
term “democracy” connotes a broad range of electoral and governmental 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
arrangements, and requires—in addition to open, free, and periodic elec-
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=61) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tions where opposition parties also have a reasonable chance to win—the 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rule of law, an independent judiciary, accountability, and a civil society.24 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=84) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Second, historically many models of economic development have led to 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
democracy.25 Third, although the theory assumes a link between capital-
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=108) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ism and democracy, this is not necessarily the case.26 Fourth, it is not clear 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=120) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that the goal of economic development is necessarily democracy.27 Fifth, 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=132) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
modernization theory was formulated without substantial direct reference to 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the experiences of the non-Western political or economic systems.28 Finally, 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=156) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
economic development can also lead to dictatorships. Despots and dictators 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can get elected by minimally democratic means, and though their policies 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and actions can lead to further economic development, they can also disrupt 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
or short-circuit the modernization cycle to the point where human rights 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
are violated, not respected. 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=217) width=121.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
This latter observation has led to the enunciation of an alternative to 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=19 y=229) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
modernization theory: authoritarian development, or economic develop-
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=241) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ment without human rights, where prosperity is valued over freedom.29 As 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=252) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with the Asian Values debate regarding human rights, discussed below, the 
page=22 block=21437064 (x=1 y=265) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
origins of the theory of authoritarian development have been traced to Lee

Kwan Yew.30 Although this theory has been criticized as being based on post

the face of economic crises34 and to thereby suppress dissent.

And  as  with  modernization  theory,  authoritarian  development  has 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=19 y=85) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

ity surrounding the negative aspects of the applied theory—environmental

page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=204) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
less the model is attractive to leaders of underdeveloped countries because 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=253) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

Neoliberalism is an economic theory associated with the writings of 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=19 y=277) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Austrian political philosopher Friedrich von Hayek and championed by 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=289) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Milton Friedman. The prestige and credibility of the theory was enhanced 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=301) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
when both men won the Nobel Prize in economics (Hayek in 1974 and 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=313) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Friedman in 1976).38 The tenets of the theory assume that human well-being 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=324) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can best be advanced by liberating individual entrepreneurial freedoms within 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=337) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
an institutional framework emphasizing strong private property rights, free 
page=23 block=21438216 (x=1 y=349) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

A Geography of Human Rights Abuses
page=24 block=866648070 (x=87 y=1) width=152.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
1067
page=24 block=399769606 (x=305 y=1) width=20.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=1 wordint.max=18
markets, and free trade. As such, the role of the state is to guarantee the 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=25) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
quality and integrity of money and establish the military, defense, police, and 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
legal functions required to secure private property rights and to guarantee, by 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
force if necessary, the proper functioning of markets. If markets do not exist, 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for example for pollution credits or carbon trading, it is the obligation of the 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
state to create them. Once created, these markets should not be interfered 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
with by regulators second-guessing markets. Government should get out 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of the business of supplying services, all areas of social provision should 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be privatized, and all sectors of the economy should be deregulated. The 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
proper functioning of the resulting system relies on personal responsibility.39
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=132) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
The adoption of neoliberalism by authoritarian governments—frequently 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=19 y=145) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as part of IMF “adjustment” policies40—can, however, be associated with 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=156) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gross violations of human rights, increasing income distribution disparities, 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and high, short-term social costs, especially for migrants.41 Thus, one can 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=180) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
point to another of the many paradoxes of human rights abuses and the 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
process of democratization: democracy allows for an easier acceptance 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of neoliberalism,42 so neoliberal ideology presents itself as pro-democracy 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=216) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and pro-human rights.43 At the same time, however, it can be characterized 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=228) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
as remote from human rights concerns.44 The case can also be made that 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=240) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
since neoliberalism became the preferred framework for restructuring global 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=253) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
capitalism, it has led to the emergence of authoritarianism despite neoliberal 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=265) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
beliefs that authoritarianism hinders economic development.45 Furthermore, 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=276) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the neoliberal emphasis on reducing the size and scope of state agencies 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=289) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
devoted to meeting social needs has the potential to weaken the very groups 
page=24 block=21438222 (x=1 y=301) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that seek to implement human rights.46

HUMAN RIGHTS QUARTERLY
page=25 block=820772870 (x=101 y=1) width=124.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Vol. 34
page=25 block=562561030 (x=296 y=1) width=29.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and therefore a preferred way of measuring its impact.47 Thus, any debate 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=24) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
regarding any link between globalization and human rights abuses suffers 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
from conceptual, methodological, and measurement problems and results 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in variable and fragile conclusions.48 Moreover, it is not clear how the con-
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=60) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
temporary globalization differs from previous ones.49 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=72) width=231.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Nevertheless there have been attempts to specify as precisely as possible 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=19 y=85) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
how globalization might be affecting human rights. At one extreme, globaliza-
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=97) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tion with its emphasis and reliance on rapid communications50 can speed up 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=108) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
diffusion of the ideas of human rights, make it easier to expose human rights 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
infractions, and conduct global consumer campaigns against abuses such as 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
abusive labor practices. By eroding geographical remoteness,51 globalization 
page=25 block=21434760 (x=1 y=144) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
can theoretically expedite the creation of an international civil society. At

HUMAN RIGHTS QUARTERLY
page=27 block=820772870 (x=101 y=1) width=124.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Vol. 34
page=27 block=562561030 (x=296 y=1) width=29.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Exceptionalism was described by Freud71 as a condition whereby an in-
page=27 block=21435912 (x=19 y=24) width=306.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
dividual feels that he or she may claim an exemption from the conventional 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
constraints of society because he or she has either suffered enough or has 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
been subjected to extenuating circumstances. Just as individuals can make 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the claim of exceptionalism to avoid respecting human rights,72 so too can 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=72) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
countries. For example, the United States is widely seen as a country that 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
makes the exceptionalist claim that its 200 years of history entitles it to its 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
own foreign policies and to forge its own approach to ratifying and imple-
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=109) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
menting human rights.73 If the United States can make such a claim other 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=120) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
countries and regions can do the same,74 and they can use the same variety 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=132) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of rationales to make their exceptionalist claim.75 The same reasoning can 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=144) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
also apply to cultures—for example, Asian or Islamic values. 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=157) width=267.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Starting in the 1980s and accelerating as the Cold War came to an end, 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=19 y=169) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Asian and Islamic political leaders and public intellectuals began calling 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
for an alternative to Western human rights as embodied in the Universal 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Declaration of Human rights (UDHR), which they felt was fundamentally 
page=27 block=21435912 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
flawed. Both Asian and Islamic perspectives point to the Western emphasis

A Geography of Human Rights Abuses
page=30 block=866648070 (x=87 y=1) width=152.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
1073
page=30 block=399769606 (x=305 y=1) width=20.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=1 wordint.max=18
the adoption of authoritarian capitalism and any discussion of Asian or Is-
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=25) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
lamic values.93 Finally, modernization is not equivalent to Westernization.94
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=36) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
In  summary, as an  explanation  for the persistence of human  rights 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=19 y=49) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
abuses, exceptionalism is a debatable basis for either rejecting UDHR and 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
substituting non-Western alternatives or for justifying outright violations of 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights. In many ways the exceptionalist argument on human rights can 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
be rephrased as “a human right is what the local despot says it is.”95 Asian 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=96) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and Islamic arguments for particular, idiosyncratic, narrow, and culturally-
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=109) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
based human rights often represent but one often extreme or oversimplified 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
position within a broader set of complex and changing traditions.96 Such 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=132) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
arguments are at best excuses for rejecting the basic premises of modern 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights—individualism, humanism, and rationalism.97 Exceptionalism 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=156) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
may result inadvertently: the European emphasis on public reasoning can 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
force the rest of the world to ignore or forget its own experiences with such 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
reasoning.98 Furthermore, universal human rights are designed to protect 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=192) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the most vulnerable among us from the disabling and degrading suffering 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that result from the most threatening features of both modernization and a 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
powerful state.99 At a minimum then, universal human rights are necessary 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=228) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
because they force us to ask how we can balance what justice requires with 
page=30 block=21438822 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
what those in power demand.100

cess to jobs, economic growth, rule of law, accountability, and the end to

page=34 block=21439122 (x=19 y=49) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Africa differ from those in Latin America and the Middle East. Given the 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
great ethnic and religious diversity in African countries, the general goals of 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
democracy have been ignored in favor of attempts to ensure national unity, 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stability, and order.128 Furthermore, newly independent African states lack 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=96) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the administrative staffs and trained personnel to manage new societies; in-
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=109) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
stead old authoritarian structures inherited from imperial power129 have been 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=120) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
adapted and legitimized.130 Attempts after 1989 to change the structure of 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=132) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
regimes and avoid ethnic manipulation131 have failed because new political 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=144) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
parties have not involved those outside elite circles 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=157) width=225.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Valuable natural resources, in high demand on world markets, have also 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=19 y=169) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
been implicated in African human rights abuses.132 This issue is virtually the 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=180) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
same as the one known as the “Curse of Oil” in the Middle East.133 It seems 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=192) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
that dictators and monarchs can get rich by mining or otherwise harvest-
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=205) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing raw materials and using the income to monopolize all instruments of 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
power—the army, police, intelligence services—while avoiding all transpar-
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=229) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ency or power sharing. Power is maintained, and the threat of a coup d’état 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
prevented, by repression, buying off opponents,134 high levels of violence,135 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=252) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and massive spending on direct services, subsidies, or government service 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=265) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
employment.136 As a result, all civic and governmental institutions remain 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=276) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
weak if they exist at all. As Friedman understands this process, natural re-
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=289) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sources obviate the need to raise governmental funding through taxation. 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=301) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
If governments have to tax their citizens they need to develop real institu-
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=313) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tions, guarantee property rights, establish independent courts, develop an 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=325) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
education system, engage in foreign trade, attract foreign investment, and 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=337) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
permit freedom of thought.137 According to Amy Ross, in addition to allow-
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=348) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ing rulers to co-opt citizens via direct cash payments to subjects, natural 
page=34 block=21439122 (x=1 y=361) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
resource wealth eliminates the need to make difficult decisions about more

page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=25) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
trade, which stimulates innovation.138 A related economic argument sees 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=36) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=48) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=61) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=108) width=133.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
As for the Former Soviet Union, its successor states, and more broadly, 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=19 y=121) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the countries where some version of Marxism or Socialism is the dominant 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
political framework, institutional constraints that lead to human rights abuses 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=145) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
are traced back to varied sources. First, a Russian history of violence applies 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
both to extremist governments, such as those of Ivan IV or Peter the Great, 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and to the peasantry that engaged in massacres and pogroms.141 Second, the 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=180) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
nineteenth century did not provide a long enough liberal tradition to counter 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
totalitarian rule.142 Third, the French Revolution and World War I exposed 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=204) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
both the population and politicians of Russia to cruelties and cheapened 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the value of human life.143 Fourth, the nature and direction of revolution in 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=228) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Russia was influenced by Serge Nechaev and Lenin, among others. Nechaev 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
assumed that revolution would require the quick and merciless destruction of 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=253) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
all those who had created a “sick society” in Russia,144 while Lenin espoused 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=264) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the notion of dehumanizing and animalizing those who were considered 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=277) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
enemies and thus expendable.145 With time, the concept of “enemy” would 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=288) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
also become an elastic category, so that executors would become victims 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=301) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and new  groups  could be eliminated.146 This approach  to dealing with 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=312) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
enemies as well as the idea of endless revolution would also be taken up 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=325) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
enthusiastically by communist parties in China and Southeast Asia.147 Fifth, 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=336) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Russian Revolution created a situation of crisis where the possibility of 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=349) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the state disintegrating was real, so terror was used to insure a strong, stable 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=361) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
state.148 Sixth, once the Bolsheviks seized power, Lenin imposed a policy 
page=35 block=21439422 (x=1 y=372) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of violence on the party.149 No overt signs of opposition to this policy were

A Geography of Human Rights Abuses
page=36 block=866648070 (x=87 y=1) width=152.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
1079
page=36 block=399769606 (x=305 y=1) width=20.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=1 wordint.max=18
expressed by other parties or social movements.150 Even the leadership of 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=24) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
the Russian Orthodox Church capitulated to the pressure of the state and 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
opted not to fight even for the protection of human dignity.151 Finally, the 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=48) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
intellectual framework of Marxist-Leninism contributed to the continuing 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=61) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
culture of violence in two ways. First, Marxist-Leninism was enveloped in 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=73) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
an aura of “Scientism” based on Social Darwinism.152 Thus the imposed 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=84) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
institutional constraints and accompanying human rights abuses were por-
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=97) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
trayed as having a scientific basis. Second, Marx and Engels promoted a 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
strong anti-liberal tradition in Western thought,153 and this perspective would 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=120) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
have a lasting impact not only on Russia, but on China, Laos, Cambodia, 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and Vietnam as well.154
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=144) width=101.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
These constraints remained in place after the fall of the Soviet Union in 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=19 y=157) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
1991. In reality the communist system was not dismantled and the heads 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
of successor states, despite having been duly elected, were in fact former 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
regional rulers.155 To make matters worse, nongovernmental human rights 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=192) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and civil society organizations, both domestic and foreign, have been so 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
marginalized that they are limited as to what actions they can take to re-
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=217) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
duce constraints to human rights. This has presented Russian leaders with a 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=229) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
dilemma: although they would like to protect ethnic Russians living in the 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
former Soviet republics, they are helpless to do so given past human rights 
page=36 block=21438792 (x=1 y=253) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
policies.156 And the repatriation of some 30 million Russians is not seen as

HUMAN RIGHTS QUARTERLY

page=37 block=562561030 (x=296 y=1) width=29.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Bold wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
where democratic processes permit a majority to elect a government com-
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=25) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
mitted to abolishing democracy, leads those concerned about human rights 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=37) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
to ask why authoritarian or autocratic governments would permit free elec-
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=49) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
tions in the first place.162
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=60) width=107.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Irregular or paramilitary forces as well as off-duty death squads have 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=19 y=73) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima-Oblique wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
been implicated in “disappearances,” extrajudicial  killings, kidnappings, 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rapes, threats, forced labor, political assassinations, indiscriminant attacks by 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=97) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
guerillas, and the murder of judges, prosecutors, journalists, and politicians 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=109) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
in Latin America163 and Africa.164 During the wars in Bosnia-Herzegovina, 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=120) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
irregulars would  blockade  shipments of food and  supplies for cities.165 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=132) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
Whether these fighters act on behalf of powerful landowners or business-
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=145) width=324.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
men or for their own revolutionary purposes, they not only target civilians 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
but also local and international human rights workers and representatives of 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
NGOs.166 The presence of irregular or paramilitary forces presents govern-
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=180) width=324.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
ments with multiple political human rights related challenges. Regardless of 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
who commits the human rights abuse, the national government inevitably 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=205) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
gets blamed for not protecting its vulnerable populations, whether they be 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
landless peasants or indigenous groups. They are also faulted for not being 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=229) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
able to control the guilty perpetrators. Such abuses bring into question the 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
sincerity and seriousness of governments claiming to respect human rights 
page=37 block=21436422 (x=1 y=253) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
and trying to improve their own human rights record.

ing human rights so long as this work is carried out in a culturally sensitive 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=25) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
manner.175 This position has become increasingly problematic, however. It 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=36) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
is becoming apparent that academic and professional organizations and 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=49) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4

viduals or groups suffering from human rights abuses, lest doing so might

page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=85) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
country at some future date.176
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=96) width=133.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
A more accommodating position acknowledges that although the West 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=19 y=109) width=309.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
cannot single-handedly confront other cultures, international human rights 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=121) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
organizations do  have a role to  play in  supporting  local human  rights 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=133) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
groups.177 They can gather, publish, and disseminate documentation on a 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=144) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
country-by-country basis on the status of human rights. They can provide 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=157) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
testimony to international organizations on the state of human rights, offer 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=169) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
training for human rights and labor leaders, members of the police, the 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=181) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
military, the judiciary, local elites, and politicians, and they can work to 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=193) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
rebuild justice systems and encourage the rule of law.178 For their part, local 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=204) width=327.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
human rights groups are charged with the same challenges and goals they 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=217) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
would have assumed in the absence of international assistance. Added to

this list would be working at issue creation and agenda setting within the 
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=241) width=327.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
elite, proposing new human rights legislation as well as institutional pro-

over human rights issues, and monitoring state compliance with human

rights obligations.179
page=39 block=21438522 (x=1 y=288) width=86.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=Optima wordint.min=2 wordint.max=4
